6c

Case simplicity

7 answers
Judgement62C-7956D-0270D-0366D-0460D-0564D-0666
Group judgement

Risks

B622nd of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for the next 18 months are more predictable than usual for a capital-goods company on the lines that reach earnings per share, and less predictable than usual on cash and on the share price. The two earlier sets are congruent and the new evidence strengthens them: revenue is contracted, margins are mechanically rising from a backlog priced in 2024 to 2026, segment margins are already at target, and the one acquired strength the FY2027 numbers need (the large-turbine ramp from 35 to 50 units) is on track and observable quarter by quarter. The drivers of the forecast's difference from history are concentrated on one contractually underpinned factor with five observable sub-conditions; the company discloses enough to monitor that factor; and the trace from its stated milestones to EPS is short and mostly disclosed. The FY2027 EBITA cone is about ±7% with its mode above the base, and the EPS cone about ±8%. The Consumer can carry the second set's FY2027 EPS of €6.54 (model) or €6.58 (adjusted) as a high-confidence median.

The three things that make the case less predictable are, in order of weight: the share price, whose multiple is two-thirds industry-driven and has become a 1.5-beta proxy for an AI-power cohort in a risk-off regime, so that FY2027 delivery cannot on its own re-rate the stock; cash, where the working-capital flow depends on the sign of orders minus revenue against a prepayment pool now €28.1bn, giving a FY2027 cone of ±27% and a negative skew from FY2028, which the market is starting to capitalise before it appears; and a short list of discrete negative tails (an SG 14 offshore finding, a casting or labour slip, a data-centre deferral, separation friction) whose combined probability is perhaps one in three and whose size is larger than the continuous upside, so that the mean outcome sits slightly below the mode. Two inputs are binary rather than continuous (the royalty's stop date and the buyback pace) and 11 November should resolve both.

The steer for the memo: present FY2027 to FY2028 EPS as the most predictable part of the case and say why (contracted volume, booked price, observable ramp); present free cash flow as the least predictable financial line and say that the normalisation is a timing question with a larger pool than previously assumed; present the share price as depending on a cohort multiple that the company's delivery does not control, with 11 November as the one near-term event that could make the multiple company-driven again. The riskiness of the forecasts is low; the riskiness of the expectations attached to them is high.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

C-569th of 19

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: The perimeter is wide today:

  • Four segments.
  • Over 90 countries.
  • Product lines from gas turbines and services to HVDC, transformers, switchgear, compressors, steam turbines, onshore and offshore wind, and hydrogen.

It is narrowing, to three segments after the Transformation of Industry separation (mid-2027 decision) and to a single brand. Predictability improves somewhat. But the largest source of unpredictability, wind (24% of revenue, near-zero margin, warranty tails), remains inside the perimeter.

Reasoning:

  • Narrowing removes a transactional, process-industry cycle and simplifies reporting. It makes comparison with GE Vernova easier.
  • It also concentrates the group in two correlated drivers (power demand for gas and grid). That raises correlation risk: our risk file notes failure clusters across gas and grid supply chains.
  • India consolidation adds a geography and a minority structure.
  • Net for predictability:
  • FY2027 to FY2028 earnings predictability is high for gas and grid and low for wind.
  • Our environment tilt (Fragility Double Underweight) penalises exactly this multi-part structure.
D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

B702nd of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: About four-fifths of the FY2026 to FY2028 earnings step rests on strengths the business has demonstrated in the last eight quarters: a priced €162bn backlog, segment margins already at the FY2028 targets, a capacity ramp of which the first half (medium turbines from 50 to 80 units, three grid plants) is delivered, pricing discipline shown in the numbers, and a balance sheet funded by customers. About one-fifth rests on strengths still to be proved: shipping 50 large turbines in FY2027 and 100 medium units by FY2028 through a casting and labour chain the company does not control; sustaining wind profit on a platform that has just had two incidents; and running a carve-out, a rebrand and an acquisition in the same year the capacity ramps land. The record is the mirror image of the risk: two outstanding years in gas, grid and cash; a five-year record that failed exactly where quality and organisational bandwidth were tested. The expected change is plausible on demonstrated strengths for FY2027 and plausible with a dependence on acquired strengths for FY2028.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • The bridge, sorted by what has been demonstrated. The second set's €3.8bn EBITA increase from FY2026 to FY2028 decomposes as: volume on the existing backlog at FY2026 segment margins about €1.9bn (demonstrated: contracted, 87% of FY2027 revenue covered, deliverable through capacity already commissioned or brownfield); price and mix already in the backlog about €0.6bn to €0.8bn (demonstrated: booked at about 20% higher gas pricing and about 300bps a year higher grid backlog margins; the pre-close confirmed new orders above the executed backlog's margins); fixed-cost absorption about €0.5bn to €0.7bn (partly demonstrated: SG&A fell from 9.8% to 8.9% of revenue in FY2025 while headcount rose 7,000); wind from €14m to €371m (not demonstrated: one profitable quarter since FY2022, and the Q3 FY2026 €75m came with 77% lower orders); the royalty saving €250m (mechanical, but the stop date is undisclosed); India (contractual). So roughly €3.0bn to €3.2bn of the €3.8bn is demonstrated, and €0.6bn to €0.8bn is to be earned.
  • Capacity: half delivered, half pending. Delivered: medium turbines 50 to about 80 units in FY2026; the Wollsdorf wind-transformer plant built in 13 months (early 2026); the Zagreb tank joint venture; the Nuremberg expansion under way. Pending: large turbines 35 to 50 in FY2027 (about 6 GW of added shipping capacity, Deutsche Bank), the Charlotte restart in early 2027, medium units to 100 by FY2028, and 50% more transformer and switchgear capacity by 2030. The large-turbine ramp matters most inside the window: roughly €1.5bn to €2bn of the €2.5bn FY2027 Gas Services revenue increase and about €0.3bn to €0.4bn of EBITA depend on it. A two-quarter slip would cost about 4% of FY2027 group EBITA and defer, not destroy, the revenue.
  • Supply: the strengths to be acquired. Hot-section castings: Howmet holds more than half of the industrial gas turbine airfoil market and is booked through the late 2020s; Precision Castparts is labour- and alloy-constrained; new casting capacity arrives only in late 2028 (CCB, 15 September 2026); Yingliu has the SGT-9000HL blade in batch production and adds five vacuum furnaces by 2027; the CIC acquisition adds 25% to ceramic core capacity. Electrical steel: transformer lead times above 100 weeks; ArcelorMittal's Mardyck lines delayed to late 2026 and early 2027; EU safeguards with a €3,400 a tonne floor from September 2026. Bushings: Trench's new Charlotte plant (3,500 a year) against 68-week Swiss lead times. Labour: five retirees for every entrant in transmission, a US electrician shortfall of up to 250,000, and transformer winding operators who take years to train. The company's answers (framework agreements into the 2030s, vertical integration, more than 95% local sourcing, two US training centres) are real, but they are plans at FY2027 volumes. This is where the thesis relies on strengths it would need to acquire.
  • Wind. The turnaround is demonstrated at the level of "no longer losing €1bn a year": H1 FY2026 loss €90m against €623m a year earlier, Q3 profit €75m, on-track for FY2026 breakeven. It is not demonstrated at the level of "earns 3% to 5%": onshore has four platforms, twelve focus markets and first SG 7.0-170 firm orders not expected before FY2027 (Bernstein); offshore has the two Taiwan incidents, 48 GW of awarded European capacity waiting for final investment decisions (Siemens Gamesa's offshore chief, 24 September), and Vestas' precedent that offshore ramp-ups are dilutive in their early years. The precedent for the turnaround as a whole, Vestas, took four years from -11% to 8% to 9%.
  • Organisational bandwidth. Calendar 2027 stacks the large-turbine ramp, the Charlotte restart, the Transformation of Industry carve-out (17,000 people, a board and works-council rejection already behind it), the Omterra rebrand (thousands of entities, "several years" per the chief executive on 24 September) and the India control step, with the CFO's and Grid head's contracts ending in November 2027. Siemens AG's Innomotics carve-out took 18 to 24 months; the one precedent for doing all of this at once does not exist. This is the strength most clearly still to be acquired, and the one least visible in the numbers until it fails.
  • The record. FY2025: every target beaten by wide margins (free cash flow 370% above the original guide). FY2026: margin guide raised to 10% to 12%, then narrowed to the upper end; cash guide from €4bn to €5bn to about €8bn; Q3 profit 18% above consensus. FY2021 to FY2023: repeated wind warnings, guidance withdrawn, a €4.6bn loss, a state rescue. The beats came in a cycle in which seven of eleven energy contractors also beat and raised; the failures came where quality control and bandwidth were tested. The record therefore earns high credibility for gas and grid margins and cash, and no more than medium credibility for wind and for parallel execution.
  • Precedents (from the t-d1 report). Companies converting a priced backlog through brownfield capacity in a sold-out market have mostly delivered the margin and often been late on the volume: ABB 11% to 21% operational EBITA over 2019 to 2026 on mix and price; Hitachi Energy on track from 10% to a 15% target; NKT's Karlskrona expansion 30% to 40% over budget but commissioning on time in Q2 2027; GE Vernova ramping 20 GW to 24 GW in 2027 and 28 GW in 2028 without visible slippage so far. Wind turnarounds took longer than planned in every case.

For the Consumer: read FY2027 as resting on demonstrated strengths with one acquired strength (the large-turbine ramp) that is observable quarter by quarter, and FY2028 as resting on two acquired strengths (supply chain at full ramp, wind at 3% to 4%) that are not yet observable. The thesis does not need new competitive wins, new pricing or new demand; it needs the factory and the foundry to keep their dates, and wind not to break.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?A661st of 8full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

B602nd of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The cone is narrow for profit, wide for cash, and far wider for the share price than for either. FY2027 EBITA: €6.8bn to €7.8bn around a base of €7.27bn (±7%), with the median slightly above the base and the mean slightly below it, because the tails are asymmetric: the upside is a steady drift (run-rate margins, guide-and-raise) and the downside is a few discrete events (an offshore wind finding, a casting or labour slip, a data-centre deferral). FY2028 EBITA: €8.1bn to €10.3bn (±12%), balanced. Adjusted EPS tracks EBITA with little added noise (FY2027 €6.0 to €7.1 around €6.54). Free cash flow after tax and leases: FY2027 €4.0bn to €7.0bn around €5.5bn (±27%, three to four times the relative width of the profit cone), with positive skew inside FY2026 and FY2027 and negative skew from FY2028. The share price's cone over 18 months is about -35% to +65% from €118.68, set mostly by the multiple (D-07), not by earnings. So the case is unusually predictable on the lines the company reports first (orders, revenue, margin, EPS), unpredictable on the line the market is beginning to capitalise (cash), and least predictable on the line the memo must call (the multiple).

The cone, with probabilities (my estimates; EUR billions unless stated; the model's base figures):

Path Probability FY2027 EBITA (margin) FY2028 EBITA FY2027 adjusted EPS FY2027 free cash flow after tax and leases What has to happen
Upper 20% 7.8 (15.5%) 10.0 7.0 6.5 Grid at 20%+ in FY2027; gas services mix; royalty stops with the rebrand (€400m run-rate); prepayments keep growing (book-to-bill above 1.2); wind 2% to 3%
Base 50% 7.27 (14.7%) 9.18 6.54 5.5 As the second set
Lower, operational 20% 6.6 (13.5%) 8.0 5.9 4.0 A systemic SG 14 finding or an onshore top-up (€1bn charge in special items, 0.5pp of margin in the run-rate); a two-quarter large-turbine slip (castings or labour); separation friction
Lower, cyclical 10% 7.0 (14.3%) 8.5 6.3 3.5, then 2 to 3 in FY2028 Gas orders halve in 2027 (Morgan Stanley's 50 GW case); data-centre deferrals (24% of the turbine backlog); contract liabilities stop growing a year early; buybacks cut to €1bn to €2bn

Probability-weighted FY2027 EBITA is about €7.2bn, within 1% of the base; the mode is above it. The base case is a median, not a mean, and the Consumer should present it as such.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Why profit is narrow. Revenue is 87% covered; the two segments that carry profit are already at the FY2028 margin targets (Grid 19.9% and Gas Services 17.3% in Q3 FY2026); the FY2027 margin the second set assumes (14.7%) sits only 0.5pp above the nine-month FY2026 run-rate of 12.6% plus the known tailwinds (repricing, wind breakeven, part of the royalty), and below the Q3 print of 14.2% that management itself said was flattered. A 1pp group margin miss is about €0.5bn or 7% of EPS; the plausible operational misses (wind at 0% instead of 1.5%: €0.16bn; grid 0.5pp lower: €0.08bn; a ramp slip: €0.3bn) sum to about 8% even if all three happen.
  • Why cash is wide. The working-capital flow is set by contract liabilities of €28.1bn whose direction depends on FY2027 orders against revenue of €49.5bn. The second set's base (+€1.0bn) assumes a book-to-bill of 1.15 to 1.2 (orders about €58bn). Orders of €65bn (gas repeating FY2026's pace) would add €2bn to €3bn; orders of €50bn (Morgan Stanley's bear) would take €2bn to €3bn away and bring the FY2028 to FY2030 unwind forward. Neither is implausible: gas orders were up 61% in Q3 FY2026 and are "not expected to be repeated every quarter", and slot-reservation growth was already decelerating in May 2026 (Morgan Stanley, 12 May). Capital spending (€2.2bn against consensus €2.05bn) and cash tax (guided "slightly shy of 20%") add ±€0.3bn each. So the FY2027 cash cone is about ±€1.5bn, or ±27%, and the FY2028 cone wider still.
  • The two-point items. Two inputs are binary, not continuous, and 11 November and the FY2026 annual report should resolve them: the royalty (about €400m a year if payments stop with the rebrand; about zero before FY2030 if not: worth 0.7pp of FY2028 margin and about 4% of FY2028 EPS between the states) and the buyback pace (€3bn a year, which the second set assumes and Morgan Stanley expects, against €2bn: about 1% of FY2028 EPS). The second set's inputs are the expected values; the outcomes will not be.
  • The tails, sized. Negative: a systemic SG 14 or onshore finding (probability about 12% in 18 months; €1bn to €2bn, mostly in special items and cash, 0.5pp to 1pp of run-rate margin); a casting or labour-driven slip in the large-turbine ramp (about 15%; €0.3bn to €0.5bn of FY2027 EBITA deferred into FY2028); data-centre deferrals or cancellations (about 10% to 15%; €0.3bn of EBITA in FY2028 per Morgan Stanley's stress test, €2bn to €4bn of cash); an electrical-steel or bushing shortage (about 10%; a one- to two-quarter grid delivery slip); separation friction delaying the Transformation of Industry decision (about 30%; no earnings effect, a narrative effect). Positive: the royalty at €400m (about 55%); grid above 20% in FY2027 (about 35%); wind at 3% in FY2027 (about 20%); a faster buyback funded by a stronger FY2026 cash print (about 40%). The negative tails are fewer in probability but larger in size, which is why the mean sits below the mode.
  • What widens the cone inside the window, by date. 11 November (the FY2030 target, the cash guide, the buyback, the royalty framework); early December (the annual report's provision movements and contract-liability maturities); 24 December (the US Department of Energy rules on 69 kV-plus equipment from 24 countries); February 2027 (Q1 FY2027 gas orders against a record Q1 FY2026 comparison, the first "peak orders" print); the Taiwan root-cause findings (one to two quarters); mid-2027 (the Transformation of Industry decision and the move to discontinued operations); EUR/USD throughout (10% is about 3% of reported revenue, little margin). What narrows it: the large-turbine shipment count in Q1 and Q2 FY2027; the FY2026 wind result; a reservation-to-firm conversion rate held near 12 of 15 GW.
  • Against the earlier sets' ranges. The second set gave FY2027 EBITA ±7% and FY2028 ±12%; I keep both and add the asymmetry. The first set's C-75 range for FY2028 EBITA (€7.9bn to €10.2bn around consensus €9.3bn) is consistent. The first set's trough analysis (€78 to €95 at 11.2x) and the model's €165 at the guideline 15.47x bound the share-price cone: the multiple range (11x to 17x on 24-month-forward EPS of about €8.2) is a ±25% band on an earnings line that moves ±8%.
  • Peers. The dispersion of outcomes after order peaks (t-d2 report) was concentrated in cash and new-order margins, not in the following two years' reported earnings: GE Power's reported profit fell more slowly than its cash; Vestas' contract assets built before its write-down. The same shape applies here, which is why I put the FY2027 cone on cash at three to four times the profit cone.

For the Consumer: the predictability of the financial forecasts over 18 months is high for EBITA and EPS (a ±7% to ±8% cone with a mode above the base), medium-low for free cash flow (±27%, turning negative in skew from FY2028), and low for the share price. The memo should state the base case as the median path and name the two binary inputs and the four negative tails explicitly, because they, not the continuous variables, will decide whether FY2027 lands above or below the base.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

B642nd of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: Helpful on the drivers that decide profit, unhelpful on the drivers that decide cash and the post-2028 margin, and the next six months are the richest disclosure window of the cycle: 11 November brings the FY2027 guide, the FY2030 framework and the capital-allocation update at once; the annual report in early December brings the provision movements, the brand-fee line and the contract-liability split; the late-December pre-close call and the February Q1 print then give the first readings on the large-turbine ramp and on gas orders against the record comparison. The Consumer can monitor about four-fifths of the thesis from public disclosure quarter by quarter. The fifth that cannot be monitored (backlog pricing, reservation-fee cash, escalation formulas, warranty utilisation schedules, the royalty's stop date, the Transformation of Industry terms) is concentrated on the cash and the post-2028 lines, which is exactly where the cone is widest (D-04).

What is disclosed, and how it maps to the thesis:

The six-month calendar of readings, and what each resolves:

  • 11 November 2026. FY2026 actuals (the second set expects Q4 profit before special items of about €1.41bn, 11.4%; wind at or above breakeven for the year; pre-tax free cash flow €8.2bn or more against "about €8bn"). The FY2027 guide (the second set expects it below its own 14.7% margin and 13% growth, in the company's habit). The FY2030 targets (the test is 20%: Morgan Stanley 20% to 22%; Deutsche Bank 20%, to 22% with the Transformation of Industry exit and the royalty; consensus 19.8%; Visible Alpha 18.9%; the second set 18.4%, about 18.9% excluding Transformation of Industry). The buyback pace. Possibly the royalty framework and the Omterra cost. This single event resolves both binary inputs in D-04 and sets the direction of FY2029 to FY2030 consensus.
  • Early December 2026. The annual report: the FY2026 brand fee in central SG&A the movement in warranty and onerous-contract provisions (additions, utilisation, releases); contract liabilities split current and non-current; special items; the Q4 working-capital flow; employee and standalone costs for the separation.
  • 24 December 2026. The US Department of Energy rules fencing 69 kV-plus equipment from 24 countries: lengthens the US grid pricing window if enacted as drafted.
  • Late December 2026. The Q1 FY2027 pre-close call: the steer on Q1 orders and on the large-turbine ramp.

Reasoning on the gaps. The opacity sits on the variables that decide the cash cone and the post-2028 margin: pricing per GW in the backlog, reservation-fee cash, escalation formulas and indexation lags, warranty utilisation, the royalty's stop date and the Transformation of Industry terms. Management's reasons are commercial (price and terms against three competitors) and legal (the trademark contract, the sale process). The consequence is that the monitoring the Consumer can do over six months confirms or refutes the profit path quarter by quarter, but confirms or refutes the cash path only with a lag of two to three quarters, through the contract-liability balance. Against peers, Siemens Energy's segment disclosure is richer than GE Vernova's on capacity units and commitments and poorer on backlog margin (GE Vernova discloses backlog margin movement), and it is richer than Hitachi Energy's and Vestas' on cash by segment.

For the Consumer: build the memo's monitoring list from the table above and from D-03's five sub-conditions. Name 11 November and the December annual report as the two readings that resolve the binary inputs, February 2027 as the reading that tests the ramp and the order narrative, and the quarterly contract-liability balance as the only public reading on cash. Expect the share price to react to the order and cash readings more than to the margin readings, even though the margin readings carry more information about FY2027 earnings.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

B662nd of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: The internal trace is clear and short for profit: stated capacity units and backlog coverage give revenue, stated segment margin targets give EBITA, a stated SG&A ratio and capex give the cost and depreciation lines, a stated buyback and payout give the share count and dividend, and the second set's model follows that chain to adjusted EPS with only two undisclosed joints (the royalty's stop date and the tax path). The internal trace is unclear for cash, because the company guides cumulative pre-tax free cash flow without the working-capital bridge that drives it. The external transmission from the value chain to EPS is clear in direction and mechanism at every step and uncertain only in its lags: value-chain forces reach reported earnings two to four years after they reach orders, which is why the forces the market is now pricing (peak orders, competitor capacity, utility affordability) will not appear in EPS inside the window, and the forces that will appear (repricing, capacity, wind repair) were set in 2024 to 2026.

The internal trace, line by line:

What the company says Operational driver Line Trace to EPS Clarity
Large turbines 35 to 50 units in FY2027; medium 80 to 100 by FY2028; backlog covers 87% of FY2027 revenue; low-teens revenue CAGR Units shipped times price per unit (about €440m per GW, Deutsche Bank); service growth on a 17-year installed base Gas Services revenue (€14.1bn to €16.6bn in FY2027) Through the Gas Services margin (18.7% in FY2027): about €0.5 of EPS per €1bn of revenue at the segment's incremental margin High on units, medium on price per unit (undisclosed), high on service attach

The chain from segment EBITA to adjusted EPS passes through net financial items (now a small positive, from interest on €11bn of cash), a tax rate of 25.5% rising to 27% as Siemens Gamesa's loss carry-forwards are used, minorities (small until India), and the share count. The second set's bridge from FY2026 EPS of €4.71 to FY2028's €8.36 (Gas Services +€1.38, Grid +€1.36, wind +€0.33, royalty +€0.23, Transformation of Industry +€0.17, share count +€0.25, financing and tax -€0.11) is the Consumer's map; every term except the royalty and wind is tied to a disclosed milestone.

The external transmission, step by step, with its lags:

  1. Power load (AI, electrification, coal-to-gas) → slot reservations and orders. Lag: months. Observable in commitments (95 GW) and quarterly orders. This is the step the market watches.
  2. Orders → price and terms. Lag: concurrent while capacity is sold out. New gas orders about 20% above the backlog; grid backlog margin up about 300bps a year; escalation clauses and reservation fees. Observable only qualitatively.
  3. Price in the backlog → reported margin. Lag: two to four years in gas (manufacturing plus 12 to 24 months of installation), two to three years in grid (lead times of 2.5 to 3 years), one to two years in services (escalators). This is the step that makes FY2027 to FY2028 earnings predictable and makes value-chain news in 2026 and 2027 irrelevant to them.
  4. Competitor capacity (GE Vernova 30 GW by 2030; Hitachi's Mississippi plant in 2029; HD Hyundai's Ulsan plant now; Mitsubishi +30% by FY2028) → new-order price. Lag: as capacity lands, 2028 to 2030. → reported margin FY2030 and beyond.
  5. Input costs (copper, electrical steel, castings) → margin. Lag: three to six months of indexation lag, then neutral; except short-cycle products and India. Observable through peer price-cost commentary (ABB's turned positive in Q3 2026).
  6. Orders minus revenue → contract liabilities → free cash flow. Lag: concurrent. The one external step that transmits fast and is not in EPS.
  7. Regulation and politics (the US fence on 24 December; affordability politics and the Ratepayer Protection Act; ERCOT's large-load rules and the Texas audit; state data-centre pauses in Oregon) → utility and data-centre orders. Lag: quarters to years. → revenue FY2029 and beyond.
  8. Peer read-across (GE Vernova's prints) → the multiple. Lag: days. Not a transmission to EPS at all, but the dominant transmission to the share price (D-07).

Reasoning. The transmission is clear because the business is long-cycle and contracted: each step has a known lag and a public proxy. The lack of clarity is in two places only. First, the size of step 2 (price per GW, backlog margin) is undisclosed, so the slope of the FY2027 to FY2028 margin path must be inferred from segment prints, which is what the second set did. Second, step 6 has no company bridge, so the cash line must be modelled from the balance sheet. Against peers, GE Vernova gives a clearer trace on step 2 (backlog margin movement, slot-reservation share) and Siemens Energy a clearer one on step 1 (capacity units, commitments).

For the Consumer: the memo can show a short causal chain from stated milestones to EPS with disclosed numbers at every joint except the royalty and the tax path. It should say plainly that the external forces now in the news act on steps 1, 4, 6, 7 and 8, none of which reaches reported EPS before FY2029, and that the forces acting on FY2027 to FY2028 EPS (steps 2, 3 and 5) were fixed when the backlog was booked.

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

D3212th of 19

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: High. The shares are a crowded, high-beta proxy for the AI-power theme in a risk-off regime.

The perception risks:

  1. AI-bubble or financing contagion. Neocloud credit stress spreads to the power-equipment group; GE Vernova or hyperscaler capex guidance disappoints. This is US bellwether read-across.
  2. Disappointment on 11 November. Morgan Stanley notes about 70% of surveyed investors already own the stock, and buy-side margin expectations sit above sell-side. A FY2030 target below about 20% would disappoint.
  3. "Peak orders" narrative. Q4 or Q1 gas orders fall year on year against the +61% Q3 comparison.
  4. Peer capacity announcements (GE Vernova raised again; Chinese or Korean entry) read as oversupply.
  5. Siemens AG placement of its roughly 10% stake, or an Ananym exit.
  6. Macro: US 10-year above 5%, oil above $100, a December Fed hike.
  7. A wind headline (a fire or a blade failure) even if immaterial.

Reasoning:

  • The share is -32% over six months, 23% below its 200-day average, with technical momentum graded D and relative strength in the 17th percentile.
  • Our environment tilt is Momentum Underweight and Fragility Double Underweight.
  • Options at one point implied a 45% probability of trading below €125. The price is now €118.68.
  • Positioning is still long (buy share 63%; Refinitiv 79% Overweight). That leaves room for capitulation selling.
  • The asymmetry: sentiment risk is largest near term even though fundamentals are strongest near term.

The record:

Period Pattern
FY2025 Revenue guide raised twice (8% to 10% to 13% to 15%; delivered 15.2%); margin 3% to 5% to 6.0%; free cash flow up to €1bn to €4.7bn
FY2026 Margin and cash guide raised at Q2 (to 10% to 12%; free cash flow €4bn to €5bn to about €8bn); narrowed to the upper end at Q3

Reasoning:

  • Siemens Energy fits the conservatism and the evidence-anchored-raise elements.
  • It does not show the precision element: beats were large, not exactly at the top end.
  • It has only two clean years (FY2025 and FY2026). FY2021 to FY2023 contained profit warnings and a guidance withdrawal.
  • The pattern is also partly a sector story (group-wide beat-and-raise across power equipment).
  • So treat it as suggestive, not confirmed. The CFO's style is explicitly conservative (sell-side "expects significant fiscal-year upsides").
  • For forecasting: initial FY2027 guidance on 11 November is likely to be set below what the backlog supports. Consensus should be built from the backlog, not from the guide.

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

  • Last year:
  • Q3 FY2026: profit before special items +18% against consensus (€1,623m against about €1,371m to €1,380m); orders +6.4%; revenue +2%; pre-tax free cash flow +3%; Gas Services margin +160 to 180bps; Gamesa profit €75m against €17m to €18m.

P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

A661st of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: The record earns high weight for FY2027, medium-high weight for FY2028 and low-to-medium weight for FY2029 to FY2030, and the record is split by business, so the lean must be split too. Lean margins up in Gas Services and Grid in FY2027 to FY2028 (the record since FY2024 is large beats on conservative guidance); lean wind revenue and margin down (the record is repeated misses); lean free cash flow down from FY2027 (cash is the most cyclical line and the least disclosed); lean reported revenue mildly down (currency and conversion speed); and lean FY2029 to FY2030 margins down against consensus and the coming FY2030 targets. At group EPS level, these leans roughly cancel in FY2027 to FY2028 and point down in FY2029 to FY2030. I have built these leans into the base case, so the residual skew around my figures is: FY2027 EBITA mildly up, FY2028 balanced, FY2029 to FY2030 balanced to mildly down, FCF down in every year from FY2027.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • The recent record (FY2025 to FY2026) is outstanding. FY2025: revenue guidance 8% to 10% raised twice to 13% to 15% (delivered 15.2% comparable); margin guidance 3% to 5% (delivered 6.0%); free cash flow guidance up to €1bn (delivered €4.7bn pre-tax). FY2026: margin guidance raised to 10% to 12%, then narrowed to the upper end; pre-tax FCF guidance €4bn to €5bn raised to about €8bn. Q3 FY2026: profit before special items €1,623m, 18% above consensus; Gas Services margin 160 to 180bps above; Siemens Gamesa €75m against €17m to €18m expected. The November 2025 Capital Markets Day segment targets for FY2028 (18% to 20% for Gas Services and Grid) were already reached by Grid in Q3 FY2026.
  • The longer record is poor and its failures were concentrated. FY2021 to FY2023 brought repeated wind profit warnings, guidance withdrawal in June 2023, €2.2bn of quality charges and a €4.6bn net loss. The failures were wind and capital allocation, not gas and grid.
  • Part of the record is the cycle. Seven of eleven energy contractors beat and raised in the same months; GE Vernova, Prysmian, ABB and Vestas also beat. Beats in a supercycle prove less than beats in a downturn. The only real downturn test in the record (wind 2022 to 2023) was failed.
  • The latest misses point to the variables that matter next. In Q3 FY2026 the misses were Grid orders (3% to 5% below consensus) and wind orders (26% below). Orders, not execution, are where the next disappointment is most likely, and orders drive cash before they drive revenue.
  • Disclosure limits. Management will not give backlog price per GW, reservation-fee cash, escalation formulas or warranty utilisation. Forecast weight on FCF must therefore be lower than on EBITA, regardless of the record.

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

A692nd of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for the next 18 months to four years are strong, unusually well underpinned in profit and weaker in cash. The model rests on three mechanical engines that need execution but not new demand: conversion of a €162bn backlog through capacity that is already being installed, repricing as old contracts ship out, and self-help in wind and in the brand royalty. They take EBITA from €2.4bn (FY2025) to €9.2bn (FY2028) and €12.1bn (FY2030), and adjusted EPS from €1.63 to €8.36 and €11.27. On FY2027 to FY2028 EBITA and EPS my base is close to consensus, so the earnings line offers little forecast edge in either direction; the composition differs (lower revenue, higher margin), which tells the third set to watch segment margins and wind revenue rather than group profit.

The edge, and the main risk, lies in three places. First, cash: the prepayment loop has already peaked, and FCF falls from about €6.9bn in FY2026 to about €5.2bn in FY2028 while profit rises, which puts my FCF about €0.9bn to €1.1bn a year below consensus in FY2027 to FY2028 on a like-for-like basis. Second, FY2029 to FY2030: as the repricing step is used up and grid price-cost tightens, my margins (17.6% and 18.4%; about 18.9% in FY2030 excluding Transformation of Industry) sit below the roughly 20% FY2030 target the market expects. If management sets about 20% on 11 November, it will be assuming more durable gas and grid pricing than I do; I would treat such a target as reachable only with pricing upside, and a target below 20% as realistic rather than as a fundamental disappointment. Third, discrete items with wide ranges: the royalty (€0.1bn to €0.4bn), Siemens Gamesa's provisions, the €4.5bn India payment, and the Transformation of Industry structure.

For the third set, the key test of predictability is therefore not whether earnings rise to FY2028 (they very probably do, within about ±7% in FY2027 and ±12% in FY2028), but how wide the cash cone is (about ±€1.5bn a year) and how concentrated the post-FY2028 margin is on one variable, the price of orders booked from 2027. For the fourth set, the gap between my numbers and consensus is small on EPS and material on FCF, capital returns after FY2028 and FY2029 to FY2030 margins; that is where guidance, revisions and narrative are most likely to move.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

A641st of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: Below EBITA the next five years look the opposite of the last six. From FY2019 to FY2025 the lines below operating profit destroyed value: net interest cost €126m to €355m a year, tax rates were erratic (from -34.6% to 32.3%), special items were enormous (€2.2bn of quality charges in FY2023), and the share count rose from about 727m to 855m through a rights issue and a mandatory convertible. From FY2026 to FY2030 they add value: net financial items turn slightly positive, the tax rate settles at 25.5% to 27% with a cash tax rate below 20% to FY2028, special items shrink from about €0.5bn to €0.15bn, and the share count falls about 10%. Together these add about €0.6 to €0.8 a share by FY2028 to the operating gain. Two items work against: minorities rise from about €45m to about €175m once Siemens Energy India is consolidated (FY2029 onwards), and the cash cost of India (€4.5bn) and of separation and rebrand one-offs reduces free cash flow, not EPS.

Reasoning and critical evidence, line by line:

  • Net financial items: -€293m (FY2025) to +€50m (FY2026), +€100m (FY2027), +€40m (FY2028), +€20m (FY2029), +€40m (FY2030). Drivers: interest income on €10bn to €11bn of gross cash, the end of more than €100m a year of guarantee fees, interest expense of about €190m a year on bonds, loans and leases, and about €20m of pension interest. The fall after FY2027 reflects the India payment and buybacks.
  • Tax: reported 32.3% in FY2025 (Gamesa losses could not be used) and 25.4% in H1 FY2026 (regional integration now allows it). I use 25.5% (FY2026), 26.0%, 26.5%, 27.0%, 27.0%, rising as loss carry-forwards are used and profit shifts to Germany and the US; GE Vernova is guided in the mid-20s and Vestas runs at 24% to 25%. Management guides a cash tax rate "slightly shy of 20%" for FY2026 to FY2028; I use 17%, 19% and 20% for cash taxes, then 23% and 25%. That gap adds about €0.5bn a year to FCF in FY2026 to FY2028 against the P&L charge. The risk is Pillar Two and transfer pricing; a return to 30% would cost about 4% of EPS.
  • Special items and acquired-intangible amortisation (excluded from adjusted EPS, included in reported EPS and cash): about €0.35bn, €0.5bn, €0.4bn, €0.2bn and €0.15bn of special items in cash (restructuring, Transformation of Industry separation, Omterra rebrand, wind remediation not covered by provisions), plus about €0.25bn a year of acquired-intangible amortisation. Reported EPS is therefore about €4.26 (FY2026), €5.91, €7.76, €9.50 and €10.91 against my adjusted €4.71, €6.58, €8.36, €9.91 and €11.27.
  • Wind provisions: €2,474m of warranty and €984m of onerous-contract provisions at September 2025, used over about five years. Their use is a cash outflow outside EBITA (I fold about €0.4bn to €0.5bn a year into working capital). A top-up would be a special item and a cash drain; a release would flatter reported but not adjusted profit.
  • Minorities: about €45m to €55m a year until FY2028; then 49% of Siemens Energy India's net profit (about €100m in FY2029, €115m in FY2030), so €150m to €175m in total. Note for the Financial Analyst: the model's inputs have no minority line, so its EPS will be about €0.2 too high in FY2029 to FY2030 unless minorities are added.
  • Siemens Energy India, net effect: it adds about 2% to revenue and 3% to EBITA (Deutsche Bank) and costs about €4.5bn of cash, which forgoes about €0.1bn of after-tax interest; after 49% minorities it is close to EPS-neutral (within about ±1%). Its purpose is strategic and structural, not EPS accretion.
  • Share count: FY2025 year-end 855m; average 845m (FY2026), 823m, 802m, 784m and 768m, from €3.05bn of buybacks in FY2026 and €3bn a year after, less about 1m shares a year delivered to employee plans from treasury shares. The mandatory convertible (61.8m shares) has converted, so there is no remaining dilutive instrument. If the buyback stays at €2bn a year, FY2028 EPS is about 1% lower and FY2030 about 2.5% lower.
  • Dividends: €0.70 for FY2025; policy 40% to 60% of net income. I use €1.90 (FY2026, about 45% of reported profit), then about 40% of adjusted EPS: €2.60, €3.35, €3.95, €4.50 (consensus €1.94, €2.60, €3.44, €4.30).

C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

B+723rd of 19

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: Large scope, concentrated in a few big areas, consistent with strategy and supported by peer precedent. The plausible group margin path is about 14% to 15% in FY2027 and about 16% to 18% in FY2028, toward the 20% to 22% that brokers expect for FY2030.

The big areas:

  1. Gas Services pricing and service mix. New units carry 20% higher prices; aftermarket growth was 11% in Q3; the fleet grows.
  2. Grid backlog repricing and capacity (+50%).
  3. Wind from about 0% to a 3% to 5% margin target in FY2028; Deutsche Bank benchmarks a Vestas-like 25% service margin for the long term.

The smaller areas:

  • Royalty removal.
  • Transformation of Industry exit (at least +50bps).
  • India consolidation (a 23.6%-margin business).
  • SG&A leverage.
  • Interest income and buybacks for EPS.

Peer precedents:

  • ABB raised operational EBITA from 11% (2019) to 20.8% (2026) via the "ABB Way".
  • Hitachi Energy plans 10% to 15% (FY2024 to FY2028).
  • GE Vernova targets 20% EBITDA in 2028.
  • Vestas went from -11% to 8% to 9%.
  • Bruch at Linde Engineering: +700bps.

Reasoning: The two big areas are mechanical (C-47). The wind improvement is the least certain. Morgan Stanley warns that the "40% gas equipment margin" bull case is unrealistic: capacity constraints limit the doubling of revenue, and the backlog mix limits margin. Management's own language ("execution machine", "value over volume") is consistent with this path.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

B+683rd of 19

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: The engine has not decelerated. Revenue growth is accelerating and running well above the company's normal rate:

Measure Rate
Five-year revenue compound annual growth rate 7.3%
FY2025 comparable growth 15.2%
Q3 FY2026 comparable growth 18.5%

Reported growth is lower because of the dollar: +9.7% over the first nine months of FY2026, and Q2 only +3.3% reported.

The input force (power demand from data centres and the grid) is at a high level with a flattening second derivative:

  • Q3 gas orders +61% are "not expected to be repeated every quarter".
  • Grid orders show no sequential underlying improvement.
  • US utility capex growth was trimmed from 25% to 23%.
  • Hyperscaler financing is under credit stress.

Reasoning:

  • What matters for later sets is the order growth rate, which very probably peaks in FY2026, against revenue growth, which keeps rising into FY2027 on capacity.
  • Leading indicators to track:
  • Gas Services book-to-bill (below 1.0 is a break).
  • The gas market below 100 GW.
  • Grid book-to-bill.
  • Wind offshore awards in FY2027.

P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

A702nd of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: The net impact is a step-change in profit and EPS of a kind the company has never produced, built mostly on contracted volume and price, with a weaker and falling cash conversion. Against the last six years: revenue growth roughly doubles (about 11% a year against 5% to 7%); the EBITA margin triples (6.0% to 18.4%); EPS rises about sevenfold (€1.63 to €11.27); free cash flow, after a cyclical peak in FY2026, settles at €5bn to €7bn a year, far above the €0.2bn to €1.0bn of FY2019 to FY2024 but below the €6.8bn to €7.9bn consensus expects. Against consensus, my base is about 2% to 3% lower on revenue, in line on FY2027 to FY2028 EBITA, about 4% higher on FY2027 adjusted EPS (definitions and tax), in line on FY2028 EPS, about 3% lower on FY2029 EPS (and about 6% lower on FY2029 EBITA), and about €0.9bn to €1.1bn a year lower on FCF in FY2027 to FY2028 like for like (before lease payments; €1.3bn to €1.6bn against the printed consensus, which does not deduct leases).

The comparison, line by line:

Revenue growth 5.2% a year (7.3% from FY2020) 12.0%, 13.0%, 12.4%, 10.5%, 6.8% FY2027 -2.3%, FY2028 -2.8%, FY2029 -3.1% (level) Capacity additions plus price; wind flat

Reasoning:

  • Quality of the step-change. About half of the FY2026 to FY2028 EBITA increase is volume already in the backlog at today's margins and about one-third is price, mix and absorption; only about €0.5bn to €0.7bn is fixed-cost absorption that would reverse quickly (P-01). This is the most forecastable earnings growth the company has had.
  • Where the history still bites. The two businesses that produced the last six years' failures (wind, and capital allocation) are both still in the perimeter: Siemens Gamesa's provisions and quality, and a €4.5bn related-party purchase in India. Both are tail risks to my base, not part of it.
  • The asymmetry between profit and cash is the net impact the third and fourth sets most need to carry: profit rises every year to FY2030; cash peaks in FY2026 and troughs in FY2028. A market that capitalises cash flow or orders rather than EBITA will see the FY2027 to FY2028 period as a deterioration even as earnings rise 40% and 27%.

Memo topic 7, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

B542nd of 8

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Guidance, consensus and the narrative reflect our assessment unevenly, and the unevenness is the investment case's shape. For the next 12 months, formal guidance is conservative and will probably be raised (I-01), consensus EPS matches ours and will drift up with the raises (I-02), and the narrative over-discounts a risk (the order peak) that does not reach the income statement until FY2029 (I-03). That combination is favourable: the earnings line that we judge highly predictable (third set) is the one the market's expectations are set below or at, and the multiple that we judge industry-driven is the one that already prices a bad outcome.

For 18 months to three years, the picture reverses. The FY2030 target is about to be set at or above where our model lands (I-01), consensus free cash flow for FY2027 to FY2028 and consensus profit for FY2029 sit above ours (I-02), and the narrative has not quantified the working-capital unwind against a prepayment pool that is a quarter larger than any published estimate uses (I-03). The collision between our view and expectations will come on cash in FY2028 and on new-order pricing in FY2029 to FY2030, not on FY2027 earnings.

The steer for the memo: the case for the next six to twelve months rests on delivery against conservative guidance and an over-discounted multiple, with 11 November as the event that can turn the multiple from industry-driven to company-driven (a 20%-plus target, a €3bn-a-year buyback, a royalty framework); the case for 18 months to three years rests on the service annuity and the perimeter changes and must carry a cash normalisation and a pricing plateau that expectations do not yet hold. Size the position for the first and hedge the second; say in the memo that the gap between our numbers and the market's is small on earnings, favourable on the multiple, and unfavourable on cash and on the outer-year margin.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

B602nd of 8

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement: The FY2027 guidance that will be set on 11 November is likely to be set below our numbers and then raised during the year: I put the probability of at least one raise to the FY2027 margin or cash guide at the Q2 or Q3 FY2027 results (May or August 2027) at about 60%, of the guide being met at or near its top end without a raise at about 28%, and of a reduction at about 12%. The FY2026 guide (margin at the upper end of 10% to 12%; pre-tax free cash flow about €8bn) will be met or beaten on 11 November with near certainty on margin and a likely beat on cash. The FY2030 targets, by contrast, are likely to be set above our numbers: about a 65% probability of a group margin target at 20% or above (the market's bar), which on our model is reachable only with pricing more durable than we assume; so over 18 months to three years the mid-term ambition is more likely to be met with difficulty or trimmed than raised. The asymmetry is the finding: short-dated guidance is conservative and will be raised; long-dated ambition is about to be set at a level that already discounts the upside we see.

The events, one by one (probabilities are mine):

Event What is guided or set Our figure Likely outcome Drivers
11 Nov 2026: FY2026 results Margin at upper end of 10% to 12%; pre-tax free cash flow about €8bn; wind breakeven 12.2%; €8.2bn or more; wind about breakeven Met on margin (95%); beaten on cash (70%); wind at or above zero (75%) Q4 seasonality and one-off reversal (margin); nine-month cash already €7.16bn; Q4 wind depends on offshore deliveries and the Taiwan commissioning slip
11 Nov 2026: FY2027 guide Comparable revenue growth; margin before special items by segment; pre-tax free cash flow Growth 13%; group 14.7% (Gas Services 18.7%, Grid 19.3%, wind 1.5%); pre-tax cash about €7.4bn Set below us: growth "low to mid-teens" (Deutsche Bank), margin 13% to 15% or the 14% to 16% pulled forward, cash "€6bn to €7bn" or a cumulative figure; 75% probability the midpoint sits below our 14.7% The company's record (initial guides have been raised in both clean years); Ferraro's conservatism; the India payment and separation costs in the cash framing
11 Nov 2026: FY2030 targets Group margin; revenue CAGR; cash; returns 18.4% constant perimeter, about 18.9% excluding Transformation of Industry; growth about 11% a year 20% or above: 65%; 18% to 19.9%: 30%; above 22%: 5% Deutsche Bank's 20% rising to 22% with the Transformation of Industry exit and the royalty; Morgan Stanley's 20% to 22%; management's statement that gas turbine margins can exceed 20% and that Grid can exceed €20bn
11 Nov 2026: capital allocation Buyback pace; dividend €3bn a year; €1.90 for FY2026 Step-up to about €3bn a year or a doubled programme: 65% (Morgan Stanley, Deutsche Bank expect it) Net cash €8bn-plus; the India €4.5bn; whether the cash guide is framed cautiously
Feb 2027: Q1 FY2027 Confirmation — Confirmed (85%); the risk is the order print, not the guide Gas orders against the record Q1 FY2026; first large-turbine count at 50-unit run-rate
May 2027: Q2 FY2027 The habitual raise point — Raise to margin or cash: 45% at this event H1 margin run-rate above the guide; cash from prepayments still growing; wind H1 result
Aug 2027: Q3 FY2027 Narrowing or a second raise — Raise or narrow to the upper end: 55% cumulative with Q2 Q3 is the seasonally strongest quarter; the Transformation of Industry decision by then
Nov 2027: FY2027 results, FY2028 guide FY2028 guide; possibly the discontinued-operations restatement FY2028 16.5% (consensus 16.3%) FY2027 delivered at or above the top of the guide (70%); the FY2028 guide set below our 16.5% (70%) Same pattern; the restated perimeter will raise the headline margin by 0.1pp to 0.3pp if Transformation of Industry is discontinued
Feb 2028: Q1 FY2028 Confirmation — Confirmed (75%) By then the cash unwind begins to show in the working-capital line

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • Why a raise is the modal path for FY2027. The pattern is two clean years of low initial guides and evidence-anchored raises (FY2025 revenue 8% to 10% to 13% to 15%, delivered 15.2%; FY2026 margin to 10% to 12% at Q2 and narrowed up at Q3; cash €4bn to €5bn to about €8bn). The structural reason is unchanged: 87% of FY2027 revenue is in the backlog at booked margins, the nine-month FY2026 margin was already 12.6%, and the Q3 segment margins (19.9% and 17.3%) sit above where a cautious FY2027 segment guide would be set. Deutsche Bank's roadshow feedback is that management is "well-positioned to pull forward" the 14% to 16% to FY2027; if it guides 14% to 16% for FY2027, our 14.7% sits in the lower half and a raise at Q2 needs only the run-rate to hold. If it guides 13% to 15%, a raise is close to certain by Q3.
  • Why the cash guide is the trap inside the event. Consensus FY2027 free cash flow is €6.8bn after tax and before leases (about €8.2bn pre-tax). Our €5.5bn after tax and leases is about €7.4bn pre-tax. The Capital Markets Day's €20bn cumulative for FY2026 to FY2028, with about €8.2bn to €9bn in FY2026, leaves about €11bn to €12bn for two years. A FY2027 pre-tax cash guide of "€6bn to €7bn" would be a sandbag by the company's habit and still below consensus; the bear narrative would read it as the prepayment loop turning. The probability that the cash guide is read as a negative on the day is about 40%, even though it would be consistent with our model and with later raises.
  • What would drive a reduction (12%). A systemic finding on the SG 14 offshore platform or an onshore top-up (the two Taiwan incidents, root causes pending); a casting- or labour-driven slip in the large-turbine ramp of two quarters or more; data-centre deferrals under credit stress (24% of the turbine backlog; Morgan Stanley's stress test of -3% of FY2028 EBITA); a EUR/USD move of 10% (about 3% of reported revenue, which bites the growth guide, not the margin guide). Precedent: the last reductions (FY2021 to FY2023) were all wind-driven and came between scheduled results; the current guide has never been cut in a clean year.
  • The secondary question: the FY2030 ambition. Our model says a 20% group margin on the current perimeter needs gas and grid pricing to hold on orders booked in 2027 to 2028, which we do not assume (we have 18.4% constant perimeter; about 18.9% without Transformation of Industry; about 19.2% if the royalty stops with the rebrand). Management will set the target on 11 November with the Transformation of Industry exit and the royalty removal in view (Deutsche Bank's 20% to 22%), so a 20%-plus target is probable (65%) and is a perimeter and self-help statement as much as a pricing one. Over three years: raised (15%: only if 2027 to 2028 order pricing holds and GE Vernova's capacity lands late); met (45%: on the post-separation perimeter with the royalty gone and wind at 5%); reduced or quietly missed (40%: if new-order pricing erodes from 2028 as competitor capacity lands, which is our base case for FY2030 margins). The CMD FY2028 targets (14% to 16%; 18% to 20% by segment) will be exceeded and are in effect being raised through the FY2030 framework: that is a "raise" the market has already priced (consensus FY2028 at 16.3%).
  • Peer read-across. GE Vernova raised 2026 guidance in July and beat again in October with gas pricing up 50%; Nordex and Vestas raised margin guidance in Q3; ABB's price-cost turned positive. The sector's guide-and-raise cycle is still running, which raises the probability of Siemens Energy's own raises in FY2027 and slightly lowers their information value.

For the Consumer (topic #4 and #7): guidance is conservative for FY2027 and will probably be raised at least once by August 2027; the FY2030 target is about to be set at or above where our model lands, which removes the "ambition cushion" for the 18-month to three-year horizon and makes the November 2027 FY2028 guide, not the FY2030 target, the next genuinely conservative marker. The gap between our assessment and guidance is favourable for the next 12 months and neutral to unfavourable for 18 months to three years.

I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

C+463rd of 8

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: Consensus EPS for FY2027 and FY2028 is likely to be held to modestly upgraded (+2% to +5% over 18 months) on the margin line while revenue is trimmed; consensus free cash flow is likely to be cut (10% to 15% for FY2027 to FY2028) as the company's cash guide and the working-capital unwind come into view; and consensus for FY2029 to FY2030 will be set by the FY2030 target on 11 November (upgraded 3% to 5% if 20% or more; cut 5% to 8% if below). Our numbers offer no edge on the FY2027 to FY2028 EPS direction, because we are at the median (EBITA within 2% of consensus; EPS +3.5% in FY2027 for mostly definitional reasons, +0.2% in FY2028); they offer a negative edge on cash (about €0.9bn to €1.1bn a year below consensus like for like) and on FY2029 (EBITA -6.7%, EPS about -3.6% once minorities are added). The net breadth of revisions, positive for twelve months and now slowing to a crawl (+0.5% to +0.8% in the last month across years), is likely to turn briefly positive again after 11 November, stall in February 2027 on the order comparison, and then depend on whether the market cares more about the margin raises (positive breadth) or the cash and order normalisation (negative breadth). I expect breadth to stay net positive on EPS and turn net negative on free cash flow and target prices, which is the "composite signal masks component deterioration" pattern the first set warned about.

Where consensus sits against our model, line by line (FactSet median against the model):

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • The state of revisions. FY2027 EPS +1% over one month, +3% over three, +11% over six, +47% over twelve; EBITDA +1%, +3%, +7%, +32%; sales 0%, +1%, +2%, +8%; target price -3%, -2%, +10%; buy share 63%, up 6 points in three months; the dashboard's revision-momentum score 11 and earnings-momentum rating A. The weekly mean EPS has moved +0.5% (FY2026), +0.8% (FY2027) and +0.5% (FY2028) in the last month. Revisions are margin-led with flat sales, have decelerated sharply since the Q3 print, and target prices are being trimmed for the sector de-rating while estimates edge up: the sell-side is holding its numbers and lowering its multiples.
  • Dispersion. FY2028 EPS runs from €6.56 (Rothschild & Co Redburn, FactSet's "mwb research" label in the upper block; Sell, €100) to €9.50 (Barclays; Sell, €130: the highest earnings and a sell rating, which says its view is on the multiple) with Morgan Stanley and Evercore at €9.18, Deutsche Bank €8.41, UBS and Bernstein €8.44, Goldman €8.19, Oddo €7.84, Metzler €7.38 and Santander €7.27. The median (€8.35) equals ours. The dispersion is in margins, not revenue (D-03), and should narrow after the FY2030 targets anchor the slope.
  • What 11 November does to each line. FY2026: a beat on cash and an in-line margin: no change. FY2027: if the guide is 14% to 16% with growth in the low to mid-teens, consensus (14.4%) sits inside it and moves up 1% to 3% on EPS; if the guide is 13% to 15%, consensus holds; revenue is trimmed 1% to 2% in either case for currency and wind (our view). Free cash flow: a cash guide below €8bn pre-tax takes consensus FY2027 free cash flow down 5% to 10% at once. FY2029 to FY2030: a 20%-plus FY2030 target lifts the five- and six-broker FY2029 figures (+3% to +5%) and pulls FY2030 numbers (Visible Alpha 18.9%) toward 20%; a target below 20% cuts them 5% to 8% and would be the first month of negative EPS breadth in two years. Capital allocation: a €3bn-a-year buyback is already in most models (Morgan Stanley, Deutsche Bank); no change unless it is not announced.
  • Through FY2027 (February to November 2027). The pattern of raises (I-01) supports +2% to +4% to FY2027 EPS across the year, concentrated at Q2 and Q3. Against that, three events can stall or reverse breadth: the February order print against the record Q1 FY2026 comparison (a year-on-year fall in gas orders would not change FY2027 EPS and would still bring estimate trims to FY2029 and to cash); a Taiwan finding (cuts to wind and a special item); and the move of Transformation of Industry to discontinued operations in H2 FY2027, which will make reported consensus non-comparable for a quarter or two and will raise the continuing-operations margin optically by 0.1pp to 0.3pp while lowering EPS 1% to 5% depending on what is done with the proceeds.
  • The cash line is where we expect cuts. Consensus FY2027 to FY2028 free cash flow of €6.8bn implies near-neutral working capital and falling capital spending at the same time. Our model has +€1.0bn of working capital in FY2027 and -€0.8bn in FY2028 with capital spending at €2.2bn to €2.3bn (the 50% grid capacity programme and blade and vane integration). The larger prepayment pool (€28.1bn) makes the FY2027 inflow plausible and the FY2028 to FY2029 outflow larger. The sell-side's own documents show it knows the mechanism (Bernstein's €3bn to €4bn; Morgan Stanley's €16.2bn stress case; Deutsche Bank listing a larger normalisation as a downside risk) but has not put it in the numbers. Cuts of 10% to 15% to FY2027 to FY2028 free cash flow over 18 months are the most probable revision in the whole set (70%).
  • The outer years. Only five or six brokers carry FY2029; their EBITA of €11.6bn (18.3%) is 6.7% above ours and their revenue 3% above. If the FY2030 target is 20% to 22%, these numbers hold or rise and our FY2029 to FY2030 figures will look conservative; if it is below 20%, they fall toward ours. Either way, the outer-year consensus will be set by management on 11 November, not by delivery, and our edge there is a view on 2028 to 2030 new-order pricing (eroding) that the sell-side does not yet model (Morgan Stanley's "next thing the market must digest" is pricing stabilisation, not erosion).
  • Breadth and ratings. With 63% buys, two sells on valuation (Barclays, Rothschild & Co Redburn) and three holds, there is room for downgrades on the multiple even while estimates rise. Target prices (median €205, mean €195) are 65% above the price; a further round of target cuts for the sector multiple is likely regardless of estimates, which is what the 1- and 3-month target revisions (-3%, -2%) already show.

For the Consumer (topics #4, #7, #8): our assessment matches consensus on the lines the market watches first (FY2027 to FY2028 EPS), sits below it on the line the market is starting to watch (free cash flow) and below it on the years the market cannot yet see (FY2029 to FY2030). The scope for estimate-driven upgrades to the narrative over 6 to 12 months is modest and margin-led; the scope for cash-driven cuts is real. The net is a small negative gap between our numbers and consensus, offset by the likelihood that the company's own guide-and-raise pattern lifts near-term EPS consensus toward our FY2027 figure anyway.

Sellside EPS

11d

Current multiple

–not yet ratedMemo topic 11, : the Rater's score1 answer
D-0740
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

F407th of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: Mostly with peers, and increasingly so. Over the latest six-month window the equal-weighted basket of the 16 dashboard peers explains 62% of Siemens Energy's daily price variance (correlation 0.78, beta 1.48), up from 19% to 30% a year ago and 28% to 35% two years ago. The stock has become a high-beta proxy for the electrification and AI-power theme: of its -26.7% over the window, about -17 points is the basket's -11.5% times the beta of 1.5, and about -10 points is its own (the peak-orders narrative, the Q3 grid order miss, the 1 September -5% day, the GE Vernova read-across). The multiple tells the same story at the cohort level: at 14.3x 24-month-forward earnings (the 32nd percentile of its ten-year range) Siemens Energy sits with the contracted-margin AI-power names (NKT 15th percentile, Nexans 22nd, Legrand 8th, Prysmian 41st, Pfisterer 0th) and not with the automation compounders (ABB 73rd, Schneider 77th, Siemens 98th). So the multiple is about two-thirds industry-driven (the de-rating of the AI-power cohort in a risk-off, high-yield regime) and one-third company-driven (a discount to GE Vernova of about 45% on FY2028 EV/EBITA, which has ranged between 25% and 50% over 18 months, and which the market attributes to wind, to the lower US share and pricing, and to the peak-orders fear). For predictability this is negative: delivery on FY2027 earnings, which the company controls, can close the company-driven third; it cannot move the industry-driven two-thirds, which will be set by the AI-financing regime, the US 10-year yield and GE Vernova's prints.

Reasoning and critical evidence:

  • The co-movement, over time. The rolling six-month R-squared against the basket rose from 30% (November 2024) to 41% (mid-2025), fell to 19% (October 2025, when the stock was in its own re-rating on the turnaround), and then climbed steadily to 62% (August to October 2026). Beta rose from 1.0 (October 2025) to 1.9 (February 2026) and has settled at 1.5. The pattern says the stock traded on its own story through 2025 (turnaround, Capital Markets Day, buyback) and has traded as a theme since early 2026. The idiosyncratic share of variance has halved in a year.
  • Which peers it moves with. The highest correlations are with ABB (0.75), Prysmian (0.75), Nexans (0.70), Legrand and Schneider (0.68), NKT (0.67) and Siemens (0.64): the large-cap electrification and AI-power names, not the small-cap grid and charging names (Arteche 0.25, Fastned 0.22, Landis+Gyr 0.14). The beta to ABB is 1.24, to Siemens 1.09, to NKT 1.03: Siemens Energy moves more than any of them for the same news. It is the sector's leveraged expression.
  • Where the multiple sits, and why that is industry-driven. The whole sector carries a thematic momentum rating of D and a technical momentum rating of D or F (every one of the 16 names), which is the signature of a cohort-wide de-rating rather than a company event. Inside the cohort the split is clean: the names whose earnings are "contracted margin" (the cable makers, Legrand, Siemens Energy) have de-rated to the bottom third of their own histories while sell-side earnings momentum stays A or B; the names whose earnings are short-cycle and software-weighted (ABB, Schneider, Siemens) sit in the top quartile. The market is discounting the contracted-margin cohort for the end of the pricing window (a 2028 to 2030 event) and paying the compounders for diffuse drivers (D-03). Siemens Energy's multiple is therefore a cohort price for a cohort fear, with its own discount on top.
  • The company-driven third. Three things are specific to Siemens Energy and explain why its six-month fall (-32% to 23 October) was about twice the basket's: the peak-orders narrative is most acute for the name with the largest gas order growth (+61% in Q3), the stock had the most to give back after its 2025 re-rating (five-year peak 28.2x; two-year median 22.5x), and it carries the wind discount and the GE Vernova pricing gap. The valuation rating of B (P/E percentile 32) against its earnings momentum rating of A is the widest such gap in the peer table after Legrand and NKT: the market has de-rated the names whose estimates are rising the fastest.
  • What this implies for the fair multiple and the TSR (topics #11 and #12). The model's guideline fair multiple of 15.47x sits at the ten-year "prudent" median (16.8x) less a little, and only 1.2 turns above today's 14.3x; the 18-month total shareholder return of +39% the model computes is therefore almost all earnings growth, not re-rating. That is the right construction for an industry-driven multiple: it does not require the cohort to re-rate, only Siemens Energy not to de-rate further. The downside case (11.2x, the ten-year "crash" band) requires the cohort fear to intensify; the upside (the two-year median of 22.5x) requires the cohort to re-rate, which is outside the company's control.
  • What could make the multiple company-driven again. A company-specific event large enough to break the correlation: the FY2030 target and buyback on 11 November (a positive break if at or above 20% and €3bn a year), a Transformation of Industry sale at €11bn to €13bn with proceeds returned (mid-2027), a wind separation decision (after FY2027), or a wind charge (a negative break). The last time the R-squared fell to 19% (October 2025) was around the stock's own re-rating on the turnaround and ahead of the Capital Markets Day; 11 November is the next candidate.

For the Consumer: model the multiple as a cohort variable with a company overlay. The 18-month share price depends on three things in this order: the AI-power cohort's multiple (industry-driven, two-thirds), the company's own discount to GE Vernova (company-driven, one-third, closable by delivery and portfolio events), and FY2027 earnings (highly predictable, D-04). The memo's lifecycle call (topic #10) should say that the stock is in a "thesis broadening while the multiple stalls" phase: earnings momentum A, technical momentum D, a cohort de-rating that the company's own delivery cannot reverse on its own.
