Request: OWN-6

Question: How fast is Siemens Energy converting its Gas Services and Grid Technologies backlog into revenue against its capacity plans (large gas turbines from 35 to 50 units in FY2027, medium units towards 100 by FY2028, the Charlotte transformer expansion in early 2027, 50% more transformer and switchgear capacity by 2030), and what bottlenecks in hot-section castings (Howmet, PCC, Yingliu), electrical steel, bushings and skilled commissioning labour could delay deliveries in FY2027 to FY2028?

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 30 October 2026, 08:09 to 08:29 UK, Fable, richie login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Siemens Energy (ENR-DE): third set (predictability of the 18-month forecasts) and fourth set (what guidance, consensus and the narrative already reflect)

Prepared 2026-10-29 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo for Richard.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Orientation for the Consumer: read this first

Dates that frame everything below. Today is 29 October 2026. FY2026 results, FY2027 guidance and the new FY2030 targets come on 11 November 2026, thirteen days away. The earnings events inside the 18-month window (to April 2028) are: 11 November 2026; Q1 FY2027 about 11 February 2027 (AGM 25 February); Q2 FY2027 about mid-May 2027 (the company's habitual raise point: FY2026 guidance was raised at Q2, FY2025 guidance at Q2 and Q3); Q3 FY2027 early August 2027; FY2027 results and FY2028 guidance mid-November 2027; Q1 FY2028 February 2028. Pre-close calls precede each (the last were 30 June and 30 September 2026), so the next steer on Q1 FY2027 comes in late December 2026. The FY2026 annual report, with warranty provisions, the brand-fee line and contract liabilities, comes in early December 2026.

Three facts in the new AlphaSense reports that correct or sharpen the earlier sets. Carry these into the memo.

  1. Contract liabilities were €28,071m at 30 June 2026, not €22.3bn. The €22,321m both earlier sets used is the 30 September 2025 balance. Contract liabilities added €5,359m of cash in the first nine months of FY2026 (contract assets absorbed €1,469m and inventories €1,335m). The company's own operating net working capital was -€6,071m at FY2025 (-16% of revenue; -€4,107m and -12% at FY2024) and -€8,823m at 31 March 2026. The prepayment pool is therefore about a quarter larger than the earlier sets assumed: the cash cushion is larger today, the FY2026 cash print has upside against the "about €8bn" pre-tax guide, and the eventual unwind is larger and probably later than Bernstein's €3bn to €4bn (which was estimated in August 2025 on the smaller base).
  2. The brand fee is about €400m a year now, and the open question is when it stops, not how large it is. The €51m in the FY2024 annual report was paid at the floor rate (0.3% of non-Gamesa revenue, which applies at margins up to 6.5%); the rate steps to 1.2% at a 9% margin, which gives about €300m for FY2025 and about €400m for FY2026 (Jefferies, 14 July 2026; 1.2% of about €33bn of non-Gamesa revenue reproduces the figure). The fee sits in central SG&A, so segment margins are unaffected. No termination payment is due (company, 3 December 2025); the licence formally ends in FY2030 (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 26 November 2025); the Omterra rebrand is phased over about 18 months from late 2026. Whether payments stop entity by entity as the rebrand proceeds or only at the contract's end is not disclosed.
  3. Siemens Gamesa's live quality question has moved from onshore to offshore. A second incident hit the flagship SG 14 MW platform at Ørsted's Greater Changhua 4 in Taiwan (blade damage on 2 October 2026, after the 8 August fire); root causes are unknown; commissioning of Changhua 2b and 4 has slipped to Q4 2026 or early 2027. The earlier sets framed the wind tail around the legacy 4.X and 5.X onshore platforms. The platform now in question carries all of wind's profit.

The Financial Analyst's model against the second set. The model reproduces the second set's EPS to within 3% in every year and its free cash flow to within 5% from FY2027. The flagged FY2026 free cash flow gap (€28,193m against €6,870m) is a working-capital basis error, explained under D-01 and corrected in inputs-changed.md; the model's net cash line carries the same error in every year and must not be used in the memo.

Where I differ from the earlier sets, in one list. (a) The first set's C-52 held that consensus free cash flow fading to 1.0x net income "already assumes neutral working capital"; the second set disagreed, and the new contract-liability data settle it in the second set's favour. (b) The first set's "€51m" low case for the brand fee can be discarded; the fee is about €400m and the uncertainty is a stop date. (c) The first set's wind tail risk (C-81, risk 1) should be re-pointed at the SG 14 offshore platform. (d) The second set's FY2026 working-capital inflow of +€3.6bn looks conservative against nine months of +€5.4bn of contract-liability inflow; this adds upside to the 11 November cash print but does not change the 18-month forecasts. (e) The second set's grid price-cost squeeze is better insured than the first set feared (D-01, point d). (f) I keep every profit input of the second set; the only change is the working-capital line, restated on the model's basis.

Appendix Forecast inputs the Investment Analyst changed before the memo

Changed forecast inputs: Siemens Energy AG (ENR-DE), as at 29-Oct-26

Money in millions of EUR, except per-share figures; costs and outflows negative. Only the line below changes; every other line of the second set's Parts 1 to 3 stands.

Part 2. Cash flow and balance sheet inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Input FY2026 (year to 30-Sep-26) FY2027 (year to 30-Sep-27) FY2028 (year to 30-Sep-28) FY2029 (year to 30-Sep-29) FY2030 (year to 30-Sep-30)
Working capital (% of revenue) 28.5 23.2 22.1 22.4 22.8

The model's FY2025 opening balance is FactSet's receivables plus inventories less payables (€16,064m, 41.1% of revenue), against which the second set's company-basis figures (-20.9% to -10.1%, with contract liabilities netted) produced a spurious +€25.2bn working-capital inflow in FY2026 (the flagged free cash flow of €28,193m against €6,870m), so the line is restated on the model's own basis so that the year-on-year changes equal the second set's intended cash flows of +€3.6bn (FY2026), +€1.0bn, -€0.8bn, -€1.5bn and -€1.2bn (FY2030).

Working capital is the balance at the end of each fiscal year as a percentage of the last twelve months' revenue.

Note for the Financial Analyst. These percentages are a device: they are not forecasts of FactSet's own line (which will keep rising as inventories grow) but the balances that make the model's working-capital cash flow equal the second set's view. The equivalent fix, if preferred, is to keep the second set's figures (-20.9, -20.5, -16.8, -12.7, -10.1) and replace the model's FY2025 opening balance with the company's reported operating net working capital of -€6,071m (-15.5% of FY2025 revenue); apply one fix, not both. The FY2027 to FY2030 flows in the model are already right; only FY2026 and the net cash line in every year are affected. The model's net cash should then be about €8.1bn at FY2026, €8.9bn at FY2027 and about €4.5bn at FY2028 after the India payment, before the model's own dividend-timing convention.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of ENR-DE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-29 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Siemens Energy AG (ENR-DE), a German power-equipment group (to be renamed Omterra) with four segments: Gas Services (gas and steam turbines and their long-term service), Grid Technologies (HVDC systems, transformers, switchgear, grid systems), Transformation of Industry (compressors, steam turbines, electrolysers; legal separation under way) and Siemens Gamesa (onshore and offshore wind turbines and service).

Request: OWN-1

Question: What did Siemens Energy AG (ENR-DE) announce at its FY2026 results on 11 November 2026: the FY2027 guidance (comparable revenue growth, profit margin before special items for the group and for each of Gas Services, Grid Technologies, Transformation of Industry and Siemens Gamesa, free cash flow pre-tax), the FY2030 targets (margin, revenue growth, cash, return on capital employed), the capital-allocation update (buyback size and pace, dividend for FY2026), and what management said about the Transformation of Industry separation (structure, partner, timing, use of proceeds), the Omterra rebrand and the end of the Siemens brand licence fee, the Siemens Energy India 51% step, cash conversion and working capital for FY2027 to FY2028, and the Siemens Gamesa offshore incidents in Taiwan; how did the FactSet consensus for FY2027, FY2028, FY2029 and FY2030 (revenue, EBIT, EPS, free cash flow) and the share price move in the two weeks after the event, which brokers changed ratings, target prices or estimates and by how much, and what did brokers and investors identify as the positive and negative surprises? Compare the guidance with what Deutsche Bank (30 September 2026: the 14% to 16% FY2028 margin range pulled forward a year; about 20% for FY2030) and Morgan Stanley (26 October 2026: a 20% to 22% FY2030 margin target; a buyback of about €3bn a year) had expected before the event.

Request: OWN-2

Question: From Siemens Energy AG's (ENR-DE) FY2026 annual report (fiscal year to 30 September 2026, expected in December 2026) and the FY2026 results materials: what was the brand licence fee paid to Siemens AG in FY2026 (the FY2024 annual report showed €51m; Jefferies on 14 July 2026 estimated about €300m for FY2025 and about €400m for FY2026 under a fee of 0.3% to 1.2% of non-Gamesa revenue), on what date or on what condition do the payments end under the Omterra rebrand, and what one-off rebrand costs were booked or guided; what was the movement in Siemens Gamesa's warranty provisions (€2,474m at 30 September 2025) and onerous-contract provisions (€984m) during FY2026, split into additions, utilisation and releases, and what were the quality-related cash outflows for the year; what were contract liabilities at 30 September 2026 (€28,071m at 30 June 2026), split between current and non-current and by segment where disclosed, and what was the fourth-quarter movement in the company's operating net working capital; what special items were booked in FY2026 and guided for FY2027 for the Transformation of Industry separation, the rebrand and wind remediation; and what did the report say about the cash tax rate, Siemens Gamesa's tax loss carry-forwards and the expected effective tax rate for FY2027?

Request: OWN-3

Question: What is known about the root causes of the 8 August 2026 turbine fire and the 2 October 2026 blade damage on Siemens Gamesa SG 14 MW offshore turbines at Ørsted's Greater Changhua 4 wind farm in Taiwan, what has Siemens Energy AG (ENR-DE) or Ørsted disclosed about the investigations, the commissioning schedule for Greater Changhua 2b and 4 (reported by DNB Carnegie on 6 October 2026 as slipped to the fourth quarter of 2026 or early 2027) and any warranty, liquidated-damages or fleet-wide inspection consequences; have other SG 14-222, SG 14-236 or SG 15 offshore fleets in Europe or Asia reported blade, fire, bearing or other component issues in 2026; how do developers and insurers describe the platform's reliability against Vestas' V236-15.0 MW; and how have the large European offshore orders that Siemens Energy said slipped from FY2026 into FY2027, and the 48 GW of awarded European offshore capacity that Siemens Gamesa's offshore chief said on 24 September 2026 was awaiting final investment decisions, progressed toward firm turbine orders since October 2026?
